### Large Language Models for Summarizing Czech Historical Documents and Beyond
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10368v1)
- **Authors**: Václav Tran, Jakub Šmíd, Jiří Martínek, Ladislav Lenc, Pavel Král
- **Abstract**: Text summarization is the task of shortening a larger body of text into a concise version while retaining its essential meaning and key information. While summarization has been significantly explored in English and other high-resource languages, Czech text summarization, particularly for historical documents, remains underexplored due to linguistic complexities and a scarcity of annotated datasets. Large language models such as Mistral and mT5 have demonstrated excellent results on many natural language processing tasks and languages. Therefore, we employ these models for Czech summarization, resulting in two key contributions: (1) achieving new state-of-the-art results on the modern Czech summarization dataset SumeCzech using these advanced models, and (2) introducing a novel dataset called Posel od \v{C}erchova for summarization of historical Czech documents with baseline results. Together, these contributions provide a great potential for advancing Czech text summarization and open new avenues for research in Czech historical text processing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the under-explored area of Czech text summarization, particularly for historical documents.  It presents two main contributions: (1) achieving new state-of-the-art results on the modern Czech summarization dataset SumeCzech using Mistral and mT5 models, and (2) introducing a novel dataset, "Posel od Čerchova" (POC), for summarizing historical Czech documents. The paper details the creation of the POC dataset using LLMs (GPT-4 and Claude 3 Opus) followed by manual corrections.  The paper then presents the experimental setup, including model variants (fine-tuned Mistral 7B and mT5), evaluation metrics (ROUGE and ROUGERAW), and results on both SumeCzech and the POC dataset.  The results demonstrate improved performance on SumeCzech and provide baseline results for the newly created POC dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Strengths:*

*   **Addressing a Gap:** The paper tackles a significant gap in NLP research: text summarization for the Czech language, specifically historical texts. This is a relatively unexplored area, making the contribution valuable.
*   **New Dataset:** The creation and release of the Posel od Čerchova (POC) dataset is a key strength. A lack of available datasets often hinders research in less-resourced languages and historical domains. This dataset provides a valuable resource for future research.
*   **State-of-the-Art Performance:** The paper demonstrably improves the state-of-the-art results on the SumeCzech dataset using modern large language models like Mistral 7B, showing the effectiveness of applying these models to the Czech language.
*   **Methodological Soundness:** The paper outlines its methodology clearly. From dataset creation using LLMs with subsequent manual verification to the experimental setup and evaluation metrics.
*   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, making it easy to follow the methodology and understand the results.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Novelty in Methodology:** While the application to the Czech language and the creation of the historical dataset is novel, the method of finetuning the LLMs on existing summarization tasks isn't groundbreaking.  It's primarily an application of existing techniques to a new domain. The LLM's being used are SOTA LLMs, but there isn't much technical novelty in how they are being applied.
*   **POC Dataset Creation Process:**  The dependence on proprietary LLMs (GPT-4 and Claude 3) for initial dataset generation introduces a potential bias. While manual correction mitigates this, the initial LLM's training data and biases could still influence the summary style and content, if not strictly then subtly. Details about prompt engineering during the dataset creation are useful but could be expanded on.
*   **Limited Baseline Comparison for POC:** The baseline results on the POC dataset are compared only against each other, and it's difficult to assess the absolute performance level of the models on this new dataset without comparison to other possible methods or human performance. More baselines should have been included.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** ROUGE, while standard, has limitations in evaluating abstractive summarization, and particularly the quality of information retention. Although ROUGEraw is used, more human evaluation or qualitative analysis of the summaries would strengthen the evaluation.
*   **Small Size of Posel od Čerchova (POC) Dataset:** Although they have summarized the entirety of what they were capable of, the number of issues and total pages is still fairly small compared to common, modern text summarization datasets.

*Significance and Potential Impact:*

The paper has the potential to stimulate further research in Czech text summarization and historical text processing. The new dataset provides a valuable resource for the community.  The improved performance on SumeCzech demonstrates the effectiveness of modern LLMs for Czech. This opens doors for various applications in cultural heritage preservation, historical research, and digital humanities.

*Overall:*

The paper makes a solid contribution to a niche area. The creation of the historical Czech summarization dataset is the most significant aspect.  The improved SumeCzech results are positive but represent more of an application of existing technology rather than a fundamental breakthrough.

**Score: 7**

*Rationale:*

The score reflects the paper's significant contribution in a relatively unexplored area. The POC dataset and the improved SumeCzech results are valuable. However, the limited methodological novelty and reliance on proprietary LLMs, and a lack of sufficient baseline comparisons temper the overall score. The limited size of POC and reliance on LLMs for generation are significant but not crippling limitations. Further development of this area, particularly with larger and even more diverse datasets would further elevate the paper's contribution in the area of Czech Text Summarization and beyond.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Generative Cross-lingual Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis with Constrained Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10369v1)
- **Authors**: Jakub Šmíd, Pavel Přibáň, Pavel Král
- **Abstract**: While aspect-based sentiment analysis (ABSA) has made substantial progress, challenges remain for low-resource languages, which are often overlooked in favour of English. Current cross-lingual ABSA approaches focus on limited, less complex tasks and often rely on external translation tools. This paper introduces a novel approach using constrained decoding with sequence-to-sequence models, eliminating the need for unreliable translation tools and improving cross-lingual performance by 5\% on average for the most complex task. The proposed method also supports multi-tasking, which enables solving multiple ABSA tasks with a single model, with constrained decoding boosting results by more than 10\%. We evaluate our approach across seven languages and six ABSA tasks, surpassing state-of-the-art methods and setting new benchmarks for previously unexplored tasks. Additionally, we assess large language models (LLMs) in zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuning scenarios. While LLMs perform poorly in zero-shot and few-shot settings, fine-tuning achieves competitive results compared to smaller multilingual models, albeit at the cost of longer training and inference times. We provide practical recommendations for real-world applications, enhancing the understanding of cross-lingual ABSA methodologies. This study offers valuable insights into the strengths and limitations of cross-lingual ABSA approaches, advancing the state-of-the-art in this challenging research domain.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of cross-lingual Aspect-Based Sentiment Analysis (ABSA), particularly focusing on low-resource languages. It introduces a novel sequence-to-sequence approach incorporating constrained decoding, designed to overcome limitations of existing methods that rely on external translation tools. The method is evaluated across seven languages and six ABSA tasks (including previously unexplored ones), and compares the results with both smaller multilingual models and larger language models (LLMs) in zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuning settings. The paper also explores multi-task learning within the ABSA framework. The results show that constrained decoding significantly improves cross-lingual performance, especially in multi-task scenarios. Fine-tuned smaller models with constrained decoding frequently outperform zero or few-shot LLMs.  The paper concludes with practical recommendations for real-world applications, emphasizing model selection and deployment strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel elements:
    *   **Sequence-to-sequence with Constrained Decoding for cross-lingual ABSA:**  While sequence-to-sequence models are prevalent in monolingual ABSA, their application to cross-lingual ABSA, especially coupled with constrained decoding, is a significant contribution. Constrained decoding, is a valuable addition that improves performance while removing reliance on external translation tools.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:** The study's breadth of evaluation across seven languages and six ABSA tasks, many of which are compound tasks and previously unexplored in a cross-lingual context, is a major strength.  It expands the scope of cross-lingual ABSA research beyond limited benchmarks.
    *   **Comprehensive LLM analysis in ABSA:** The comparative assessment of LLMs (including LLaMA 3, Orca 2, and ChatGPT) in various settings (zero-shot, few-shot, and fine-tuning) for cross-lingual ABSA is another novel contribution.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Addresses Low-Resource Language Gap:**  The paper directly tackles the under-representation of low-resource languages in ABSA research, making the method and findings practically relevant.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:**  The inclusion of recommendations for model selection and deployment provides tangible guidance for researchers and practitioners working on real-world ABSA applications.
    *   **Benchmark Setting:** By evaluating and providing results on unexplored tasks, the paper provides a new benchmark for future research in cross-lingual ABSA.
    *   **Multi-task advantage:** The exploration of multi-task learning shows a significant advantage, especially for constrained decoding, further increasing practical value by reducing deployment requirements.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experimental design is robust and well-executed, with detailed hyperparameter settings.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized, making it easy to understand the methodology and findings.
    *   **Rigorous Analysis:**  The paper includes a detailed error analysis to identify challenges and limitations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dataset Limitations:** While the study expands evaluation, the sole reliance on restaurant review data limits generalizability. The findings could be further validated with other domain-specific datasets.
    *   **LLM Fine-tuning:** The paper shows that smaller, specialized models often outperform large LLMs, however, there may be room for further optimization in LLM fine-tuning for cross-lingual ABSA. More detailed exploration of LoRA parameters or prompt engineering techniques would be valuable.
    *   **Computational Cost of Multi-tasking:** The paper acknowledges the substantial computational costs associated with training multi-task models. Further analysis of the trade-offs between performance and computational resources would enhance the practical utility of the research.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has strong potential to influence the field of cross-lingual ABSA by:
    *   Encouraging more research into sequence-to-sequence approaches and constrained decoding.
    *   Motivating researchers to explore a wider range of ABSA tasks and languages.
    *   Providing a benchmark for evaluating future methods.
    *   Guiding the selection of appropriate models and deployment strategies for real-world ABSA applications.

**Score: 8.5**

**Justification:** The paper makes several novel contributions that address critical challenges in cross-lingual ABSA, especially concerning low-resource languages. The sequence-to-sequence with constrained decoding methodology is significant, as is the extensive evaluation across multiple languages and tasks, and the detailed LLM analysis. Its influence is further solidified by providing practical recommendations for model selection and deployment. However, the paper could be improved by exploring other domain-specific datasets, optimizing LLM fine-tuning strategies, and providing a more detailed analysis of the cost vs. benefit trade-offs when deploying multi-task models. These limitations keep it from a 9 or 10 score, but the overall contribution is substantial.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Few-shot Vision-based Human Activity Recognition with MLLM-based Visual Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10371v1)
- **Authors**: Wenqi Zheng, Yutaka Arakawa
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning in large reasoning models enables learning from feedback on their outputs, making it particularly valuable in scenarios where fine-tuning data is limited. However, its application in multi-modal human activity recognition (HAR) domains remains largely underexplored. Our work extends reinforcement learning to the human activity recognition domain with multimodal large language models. By incorporating visual reinforcement learning in the training process, the model's generalization ability on few-shot recognition can be greatly improved. Additionally, visual reinforcement learning can enhance the model's reasoning ability and enable explainable analysis in the inference stage. We name our few-shot human activity recognition method with visual reinforcement learning FAVOR. Specifically, our approach first utilizes a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to generate multiple candidate responses for the human activity image, each containing reasoning traces and final answers. These responses are then evaluated using reward functions, and the MLLM model is subsequently optimized using the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm. In this way, the MLLM model can be adapted to human activity recognition with only a few samples. Extensive experiments on four human activity recognition datasets and five different settings demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FAVOR (Few-shot human Activity recognition method with Visual reinfORcement learning), a novel approach for few-shot human activity recognition (HAR) using Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). FAVOR leverages visual reinforcement learning to improve the MLLM's generalization ability and reasoning capabilities in scenarios where limited training data is available. The method generates multiple candidate responses for a given input image, each containing reasoning traces and final answers. These responses are then evaluated using rule-based reward functions, and the MLLM is optimized using the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm. The paper presents experimental results on four HAR datasets, demonstrating that FAVOR outperforms supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and baseline methods in few-shot settings. The key contributions are: a novel method (FAVOR), a verifiable reward function for HAR, and experimental validation showing improved performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the application of visual reinforcement learning, specifically GRPO, to MLLMs for the task of few-shot HAR. While MLLMs and reinforcement learning have been used separately and in related areas, the specific combination for this task with rule-based verifiable rewards appears novel. The use of GRPO to refine reasoning traces and improve answer accuracy is also a valuable contribution. The use of rule-based verifiable rewards makes the method data efficient.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant challenge in HAR: the need for large labeled datasets. By demonstrating effective few-shot learning, FAVOR offers a promising approach for real-world applications where data is scarce or expensive to obtain. The improved reasoning and explainability (through the reasoning traces) are also beneficial for practical deployment. Furthermore, the consistent performance improvement over SFT across multiple datasets suggests the method's robustness and generalizability. While rule-based rewards work for this task, defining them for more complex tasks remains a hurdle.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-structured methodology.
    *   The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of FAVOR over baselines, especially in few-shot settings.
    *   Ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of different components of the method.
    *   The approach enhances explainability via reasoning traces.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reward functions are rule-based and might be difficult to design for more complex activities or tasks.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed analysis of the types of reasoning errors that FAVOR is able to correct.
    *   The computational cost of GRPO is not thoroughly discussed. Reinforcement learning can be computationally expensive.
    *   The reliance on a specific backbone model (Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct) limits the generalizability of the method's results. Performance may vary with other MLLM architectures.
    *   While the verifiable rewards are an interesting approach, their design is somewhat tied to the classification task and could be a bottleneck when transferring to different HAR setups. A discussion about the limitations of rule-based rewards would have been beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more data-efficient and explainable HAR systems. The combination of MLLMs and reinforcement learning is a promising direction, and the verifiable reward system could inspire further research in this area. Also, this framework might extend to other applications like video understanding, surveillance, and assistive technology. The idea of combining reasoning tokens and final answers for reinforcement also appears promising.

*   **Justification for Score:**  Despite its strengths, the reliance on rule-based verifiable rewards makes the general application of this method to novel scenarios, such as robotic manipulation with a wider space of actions, difficult. Given that the work primarily focuses on classification with only a limited amount of samples, and given its heavy dependence on the structure of the specific rewards, its overall impact and novelty do not currently warrant a high score.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 6/10

### A Semantic-Aware Framework for Safe and Intent-Integrative Assistance in Upper-Limb Exoskeletons
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10378v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Chen, Shu Miao, Chunyu Wu, Jingsong Mu, Bo OuYang, Xiang Li
- **Abstract**: Upper-limb exoskeletons are primarily designed to provide assistive support by accurately interpreting and responding to human intentions. In home-care scenarios, exoskeletons are expected to adapt their assistive configurations based on the semantic information of the task, adjusting appropriately in accordance with the nature of the object being manipulated. However, existing solutions often lack the ability to understand task semantics or collaboratively plan actions with the user, limiting their generalizability. To address this challenge, this paper introduces a semantic-aware framework that integrates large language models into the task planning framework, enabling the delivery of safe and intent-integrative assistance. The proposed approach begins with the exoskeleton operating in transparent mode to capture the wearer's intent during object grasping. Once semantic information is extracted from the task description, the system automatically configures appropriate assistive parameters. In addition, a diffusion-based anomaly detector is used to continuously monitor the state of human-robot interaction and trigger real-time replanning in response to detected anomalies. During task execution, online trajectory refinement and impedance control are used to ensure safety and regulate human-robot interaction. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method effectively aligns with the wearer's cognition, adapts to semantically varying tasks, and responds reliably to anomalies.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score with a detailed justification.

**Summary:**

This paper presents a semantic-aware framework for upper-limb exoskeletons designed to provide safer and more intent-integrative assistance. The core idea is to integrate large language models (LLMs) into the task planning and control architecture of the exoskeleton. The framework operates in three main phases: (1) LLM-enhanced task planning, where the LLM reasons about the task based on natural language descriptions, (2) intent-integrative grasping, where the exoskeleton operates in transparent mode allowing the wearer to freely grasp objects, and (3) semantic-aware assistance during the transport phase, where the LLM provides context-aware assistance, and anomaly detection ensures conflict between the user's intention and system's action trigger replanning.  The system uses a diffusion-based anomaly detector to continuously monitor the human-robot interaction and trigger real-time replanning when necessary. During task execution, online trajectory refinement and impedance control are used to ensure safety and regulate human-robot interaction. The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method aligns with the wearer's cognition, adapts to semantically varying tasks, and responds reliably to anomalies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in its integration of LLMs for semantic understanding and task planning within an upper-limb exoskeleton framework, particularly its effort to account for human intention and safety in a dynamically changing environment.  While LLMs have been used in robotics before, their application to *assistive* exoskeletons, especially with such a focus on intention-based control and semantic understanding of tasks to modulate assistance parameters and safety constraints, represents a valuable contribution. The anomaly detection component using diffusion models to ensure safe operation and enable replanning when conflicts arise also enhances the originality.

*   **Significance:**  The significance stems from the potential to improve the user experience and safety of assistive exoskeletons.  By enabling the exoskeleton to understand the task at hand (e.g., "carry a fragile object"), the system can adapt its behavior to reduce the risk of accidents or discomfort.  The incorporation of user intention throughout the process makes the system more intuitive and responsive. The anomaly detection mechanism tackles a key challenge in human-robot interaction – anticipating and reacting to unexpected events or mismatches between the user's intent and the robot's planned actions.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined problem:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing exoskeleton control systems.
    *   **Comprehensive framework:** The proposed framework addresses multiple aspects of the problem, including task planning, intent integration, safety, and real-time adaptation.
    *   **Integration of state-of-the-art techniques:** The paper combines LLMs, diffusion models, and advanced control techniques.
    *   **Experimental validation:** The paper includes experiments to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited scope of experiments:** The experiments are somewhat limited in scope and could be expanded to include more diverse tasks, users, and environmental conditions.
    *   **Lack of user studies:** The paper lacks formal user studies to assess the subjective experience of using the proposed system.
    *   **Reliance on predefined movements:** While the framework allows for semantic understanding, the assistance still appears to rely on a set of predefined movements (Table III), potentially limiting the system's flexibility in unstructured environments. This makes LLM use as a semantic extraction and subtask-selecting unit more than a true generative task planner.
    *   **Anomaly detection threshold:** It is not clearly outlined how the threshold for the anomaly score 's' is chosen, or how it can be tuned for new users and tasks.
    *   **Limited discussion of LLM safety and robustness:** The paper does not explicitly address the potential for LLM errors or biases and how these might impact safety. More discussion regarding mitigation strategies would increase trust.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence the design of future assistive exoskeletons by demonstrating the benefits of integrating LLMs and anomaly detection techniques. It could inspire further research into intent-based control, semantic-aware assistance, and safe human-robot interaction.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel integration of LLMs into the control architecture of an upper-limb exoskeleton, demonstrating improved safety and intent-integrative assistance. While the experimental validation is somewhat limited and lacks user studies, the framework is well-designed and addresses a significant challenge in the field. The anomaly detection mechanism and the context-aware parameter adjustment contribute significantly to the originality of the work. However, the dependence on predefined movements, the lack of discussion on LLM failure modes and the absence of user studies constrain the impact somewhat. For these reasons, a score of **7** is appropriate. It's a solid contribution with good novelty, but further work is needed to fully realize its potential.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Towards Spatially Consistent Image Generation: On Incorporating Intrinsic Scene Properties into Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10382v1)
- **Authors**: Hyundo Lee, Suhyung Choi, Byoung-Tak Zhang, Inwoo Hwang
- **Abstract**: Image generation models trained on large datasets can synthesize high-quality images but often produce spatially inconsistent and distorted images due to limited information about the underlying structures and spatial layouts. In this work, we leverage intrinsic scene properties (e.g., depth, segmentation maps) that provide rich information about the underlying scene, unlike prior approaches that solely rely on image-text pairs or use intrinsics as conditional inputs. Our approach aims to co-generate both images and their corresponding intrinsics, enabling the model to implicitly capture the underlying scene structure and generate more spatially consistent and realistic images. Specifically, we first extract rich intrinsic scene properties from a large image dataset with pre-trained estimators, eliminating the need for additional scene information or explicit 3D representations. We then aggregate various intrinsic scene properties into a single latent variable using an autoencoder. Building upon pre-trained large-scale Latent Diffusion Models (LDMs), our method simultaneously denoises the image and intrinsic domains by carefully sharing mutual information so that the image and intrinsic reflect each other without degrading image quality. Experimental results demonstrate that our method corrects spatial inconsistencies and produces a more natural layout of scenes while maintaining the fidelity and textual alignment of the base model (e.g., Stable Diffusion).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Intrinsic Latent Diffusion Models (I-LDM), a novel approach to improve the spatial consistency of text-to-image (T2I) generation by jointly modeling images and their intrinsic scene properties (depth, surface normals, segmentation, line drawings).  Unlike previous methods that use intrinsics as conditional inputs, I-LDM co-generates the image and intrinsics, allowing them to mutually regularize each other during the denoising process.  The method extracts intrinsics from large datasets using pre-trained estimators, encodes them into a single latent space using an autoencoder, and then fine-tunes a large-scale latent diffusion model (LDM) to simultaneously denoise the image and intrinsic domains.  A cross-domain weight scheduling mechanism shares self-attention between domains while preventing visual artifacts.  Experiments demonstrate that I-LDM generates more spatially consistent and realistic images while maintaining fidelity and textual alignment of the original LDM.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the *joint modeling* of images and intrinsics to improve spatial consistency.  Prior works have used intrinsics as conditional inputs, but the co-generation approach, allowing mutual regularization, is a distinct contribution. The use of pre-trained estimators for intrinsic extraction eliminates the need for expensive 3D data or manual labeling, enhancing practicality. The architecture that uses LoRA and cross-domain attention while preserving the quality of the original LDM model is also a novel engineering aspect.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a key limitation of current T2I models – their tendency to produce spatially inconsistent and distorted images. This is a well-known problem and a significant barrier to the broader adoption of T2I technology. I-LDM's ability to improve spatial consistency makes generated images more realistic and usable in downstream applications. Demonstrating improvements across different prompts (Parti, Multi, and a Hand pose dataset) and different architectures (SDXL and PixArt) shows strong potential.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** Quantitative and qualitative results consistently demonstrate improvements in spatial consistency, human preference scores, and maintenance of base model quality.  The comparisons to various baselines, including alternative ways to incorporate intrinsics, are compelling.
    *   **Efficient and Practical Approach:** Using pre-trained estimators for intrinsics and fine-tuning with LoRA makes the method scalable and adaptable to existing LDMs.  The weight scheduling mechanism addresses a critical issue of visual artifacts and avoids compromising image quality.
    *   **Extensive Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies validate the importance of each component of I-LDM, including the joint modeling, intrinsic VAE, cross-domain attention, and weight scheduling.
    *   **Good Qualitative Analysis:** The inclusion of several qualitative examples (and additional in the appendix) show improved visual consistency.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Estimators:** The quality of generated intrinsics is limited by the accuracy and robustness of pre-trained estimators. Imperfect intrinsics can still lead to artifacts, although the paper shows some robustness to this issue through downscaling experiments.
    *   **Increased Computational Cost:** While LoRA makes the fine-tuning efficient, co-generation inevitably increases the computational cost compared to standard LDM inference. The overhead should be acceptable, but it is nonetheless a weakness. The paper provides information about the additional cost but more detailed comparative analysis (and potential optimization directions) would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited Direct Evaluation of Spatial Consistency:** The paper relies heavily on human preference scores and LLM evaluations as proxies for spatial consistency. While these are valid, a more direct quantitative metric that specifically measures spatial relationships or object geometry could further strengthen the claims. The ablation is missing an assessment of the *quality* of the intrinsic maps that are generated. Were they a good and appropriate reflection of the text prompt? Did they help or hinder? A good downstream task like consistent 3D reconstruction would be useful.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Failure Cases:** While the appendix included failure cases, the analysis in main paper primarily focuses on success cases. A more detailed discussion of failure modes and potential remedies would be valuable.
    *   **Need more analysis on multi object scenes**: I would like to see some quantitative data on how well this approach can separate multiple objects and define object boundaries, spatial relations.

*   **Potential Impact:** I-LDM has the potential to significantly impact the field of T2I generation by addressing the critical issue of spatial inconsistency. This can make these models much better for more tasks and could open the door to applications that require structural accuracy. The method's practicality also makes it likely to be adopted and built upon by other researchers.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:**

I assign a score of 8 because the paper presents a novel and technically sound approach to a significant problem in T2I generation. The joint modeling framework, the practical implementation using LoRA and pre-trained estimators, and the strong empirical results all contribute to a substantial contribution. While the reliance on pre-trained estimators and the increased computational cost are limitations, they do not significantly detract from the overall value of the work. The I-LDM is going to be a very strong baseline for a while in the community because the results are quite remarkable compared to other approaches and it leverages very standard techniques. I would have liked some further detail on the limitations and failure cases, but this does not change my overall assessment.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Jailbreaking Commercial Black-Box LLMs with Explicitly Harmful Prompts
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10390v1)
- **Authors**: Chiyu Zhang, Lu Zhou, Xiaogang Xu, Jiafei Wu, Liming Fang, Zhe Liu
- **Abstract**: Evaluating jailbreak attacks is challenging when prompts are not overtly harmful or fail to induce harmful outputs. Unfortunately, many existing red-teaming datasets contain such unsuitable prompts. To evaluate attacks accurately, these datasets need to be assessed and cleaned for maliciousness. However, existing malicious content detection methods rely on either manual annotation, which is labor-intensive, or large language models (LLMs), which have inconsistent accuracy in harmful types. To balance accuracy and efficiency, we propose a hybrid evaluation framework named MDH (Malicious content Detection based on LLMs with Human assistance) that combines LLM-based annotation with minimal human oversight, and apply it to dataset cleaning and detection of jailbroken responses. Furthermore, we find that well-crafted developer messages can significantly boost jailbreak success, leading us to propose two new strategies: D-Attack, which leverages context simulation, and DH-CoT, which incorporates hijacked chains of thought. The Codes, datasets, judgements, and detection results will be released in github repository: https://github.com/AlienZhang1996/DH-CoT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of evaluating jailbreak attacks on commercial black-box Large Language Models (LLMs). It identifies three problematic types of prompts in existing red-teaming datasets (Benign Prompts, Non-obvious Harmful Prompts, Non-Triggering harmful-response Prompts) that hinder accurate evaluation. To improve dataset quality, the authors propose MDH (Malicious content Detection based on LLMs with Human assistance), a hybrid framework combining LLM-based annotation with minimal human oversight. Furthermore, the paper introduces two new jailbreak attack strategies: D-Attack, which leverages context simulation, and DH-CoT, incorporating hijacked chains of thought, both utilizing carefully crafted developer messages. The authors clean several existing datasets using MDH to create the RTA (Red Team datasets for Attack) series and evaluate the effectiveness of the proposed attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The identification of the three problematic prompt types (BP, NHP, and NTP) in red-teaming datasets is a valuable contribution, as it directly addresses a practical issue in evaluating LLMs' robustness. The MDH framework represents a practical approach to malicious content detection, balancing automation and human oversight, which is an improvement over purely manual or purely LLM-based methods. The D-Attack and DH-CoT methods are novel in their use of developer messages and a combination of context simulation and hijacked chains of thought, offering a potentially new angle on jailbreaking LLMs, particularly reasoning models. However, the individual components of these attacks (e.g., few-shot learning, CoT) are not entirely novel in the field of adversarial attacks on LLMs. The novelty lies in their specific combination and application in the context of developer-message-based jailbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a significant problem in the LLM security and red-teaming community. By improving the quality of red-teaming datasets, the MDH framework contributes to more accurate and reliable evaluations of LLM vulnerabilities. The proposed attack methods, D-Attack and DH-CoT, can help uncover weaknesses in LLMs, potentially leading to improved defense mechanisms and safer deployment. The creation and release of the RTA dataset series can serve as a valuable resource for the research community, facilitating further development of jailbreak attacks and defense strategies.

*   **Strengths:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the proposed methods and experiments. The use of a hybrid approach (MDH) strikes a good balance between automation and human expertise. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the MDH framework and the proposed attack methods.

*   **Weaknesses:** While the paper provides evidence of the effectiveness of D-Attack and DH-CoT, the generalizability of these attacks to other models and datasets requires further investigation. The dependence on developer messages limits applicability to models that support this feature.
    The paper may be improved by including detailed ablation analysis of different components from suggested framework to indicate which ones are more effective.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design and evaluation of future red-teaming datasets. The MDH framework could become a standard practice for cleaning and curating datasets used for evaluating LLMs' robustness. The proposed attack methods may inspire the development of new and more effective jailbreak techniques. The RTA dataset series could become a widely used benchmark for evaluating LLM security.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM security by addressing the practical issues of red-teaming dataset quality and introducing novel attack strategies. The novelty lies in a novel combination of existing techniques and its focus on developer messages. The paper's significance is demonstrated through experimental results and its potential to influence future research and development in LLM security. However, there are limitations in the generalizability of the proposed attack methods, and the novelty of individual techniques might be considered incremental rather than revolutionary. Overall, the paper presents a solid contribution with good impact and a clear demonstration of practical application, and the contribution is sufficient to justify a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### LeanRAG: Knowledge-Graph-Based Generation with Semantic Aggregation and Hierarchical Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10391v1)
- **Authors**: Yaoze Zhang, Rong Wu, Pinlong Cai, Xiaoman Wang, Guohang Yan, Song Mao, Ding Wang, Botian Shi
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) plays a crucial role in grounding Large Language Models by leveraging external knowledge, whereas the effectiveness is often compromised by the retrieval of contextually flawed or incomplete information. To address this, knowledge graph-based RAG methods have evolved towards hierarchical structures, organizing knowledge into multi-level summaries. However, these approaches still suffer from two critical, unaddressed challenges: high-level conceptual summaries exist as disconnected ``semantic islands'', lacking the explicit relations needed for cross-community reasoning; and the retrieval process itself remains structurally unaware, often degenerating into an inefficient flat search that fails to exploit the graph's rich topology. To overcome these limitations, we introduce LeanRAG, a framework that features a deeply collaborative design combining knowledge aggregation and retrieval strategies. LeanRAG first employs a novel semantic aggregation algorithm that forms entity clusters and constructs new explicit relations among aggregation-level summaries, creating a fully navigable semantic network. Then, a bottom-up, structure-guided retrieval strategy anchors queries to the most relevant fine-grained entities and then systematically traverses the graph's semantic pathways to gather concise yet contextually comprehensive evidence sets. The LeanRAG can mitigate the substantial overhead associated with path retrieval on graphs and minimizes redundant information retrieval. Extensive experiments on four challenging QA benchmarks with different domains demonstrate that LeanRAG significantly outperforming existing methods in response quality while reducing 46\% retrieval redundancy. Code is available at: https://github.com/RaZzzyz/LeanRAG
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LeanRAG paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LeanRAG, a novel framework for Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) that addresses limitations in existing knowledge graph-based RAG methods.  Existing methods often suffer from disconnected "semantic islands" (high-level summaries lacking explicit relations) and structurally unaware retrieval processes. LeanRAG tackles these issues with two key innovations: 1) a semantic aggregation algorithm that constructs a hierarchical knowledge graph with explicit relations between summary nodes, creating a fully navigable semantic network; and 2) a bottom-up, structure-guided retrieval strategy that anchors queries to fine-grained entities and traverses the graph's semantic pathways to gather concise and contextually comprehensive evidence.  The paper demonstrates LeanRAG's superiority over baseline methods on several challenging QA benchmarks, showing improved response quality and reduced retrieval redundancy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of RAG, specifically in knowledge graph-based approaches.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a clear and well-defined problem:** The paper effectively identifies and articulates the limitations of existing hierarchical KG-RAG methods, which is commendable. The "semantic islands" problem and the lack of structure-aware retrieval are important considerations.
    *   **Novelty of the approach:** LeanRAG introduces a combination of techniques (semantic aggregation with inter-cluster relations and bottom-up retrieval) that are genuinely new. The semantic aggregation algorithm is particularly interesting.
    *   **Strong experimental results:** The extensive experimental evaluation on four diverse QA benchmarks provides compelling evidence of LeanRAG's effectiveness. The ablation studies are crucial for understanding the contribution of each component.  Quantifying the reduction in redundancy is also a key strength.
    *   **Clarity of presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the proposed method and the experimental setup. The figures are helpful for visualizing the framework.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dependence on LLMs for aggregation and relation generation:** A potential concern is the reliance on LLMs (e.g., `Fentity`, `Frel`) for generating summary entities and inter-cluster relations. The quality of these generated summaries and relations depends heavily on the LLM used and the prompt design. It is crucial to consider how prompt engineering and LLM selection impact the overall performance. This aspect needs further investigation. More analysis of the generated summaries/relations, including example outputs, would be beneficial.
    *   **Limited analysis of aggregation's impact:** While ablation studies demonstrated that the inter-cluster relations improve diversity and overall output quality, a more in-depth analysis regarding the types of relations that are most impactful would enhance the contribution.
    *   **Lack of discussion of scalability:** Although the paper shows reduced information redundancy, it could benefit from a discussion regarding the scalability challenges associated with constructing and traversing large knowledge graphs, especially in dynamic or real-time environments.
    *   **Limited exploration of different GMM configurations:** The number of clusters for the GMM (clustersize) is a crucial hyperparameter. A more systematic exploration of different GMM configurations and their impact on performance would strengthen the paper.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The explicit modeling of relations between summary nodes is a significant step forward in knowledge graph organization for RAG. This addresses a key limitation of previous hierarchical approaches.
    *   The bottom-up retrieval strategy is a clever way to exploit the graph's structure and reduce redundancy.
    *   The improved response quality and reduced redundancy demonstrated in the experiments make LeanRAG a promising approach for building more effective and efficient RAG systems.
    *   The concepts presented have the potential to impact the broader field of knowledge representation and reasoning.

**Justification:**

The paper is a significant contribution as it addresses key limitations of previous graph-based RAG methods. The proposed method is novel and is supported by thorough experiments. While certain aspects of the approach such as scalability and the reliance on LLMs for relation generation could be explored in further depth, the work makes valuable strides. The ablation studies and analyses have significantly added to the contribution. The approach provides significant improvements in response quality.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### PQ-DAF: Pose-driven Quality-controlled Data Augmentation for Data-scarce Driver Distraction Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10397v1)
- **Authors**: Haibin Sun, Xinghui Song
- **Abstract**: Driver distraction detection is essential for improving traffic safety and reducing road accidents. However, existing models often suffer from degraded generalization when deployed in real-world scenarios. This limitation primarily arises from the few-shot learning challenge caused by the high cost of data annotation in practical environments, as well as the substantial domain shift between training datasets and target deployment conditions. To address these issues, we propose a Pose-driven Quality-controlled Data Augmentation Framework (PQ-DAF) that leverages a vision-language model for sample filtering to cost-effectively expand training data and enhance cross-domain robustness. Specifically, we employ a Progressive Conditional Diffusion Model (PCDMs) to accurately capture key driver pose features and synthesize diverse training examples. A sample quality assessment module, built upon the CogVLM vision-language model, is then introduced to filter out low-quality synthetic samples based on a confidence threshold, ensuring the reliability of the augmented dataset. Extensive experiments demonstrate that PQ-DAF substantially improves performance in few-shot driver distraction detection, achieving significant gains in model generalization under data-scarce conditions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Pose-driven Quality-controlled Data Augmentation Framework (PQ-DAF) for improving driver distraction detection in data-scarce scenarios. It uses DWpose to extract pose information, and then employs Progressive Conditional Diffusion Models (PCDMs) to generate diverse synthetic training examples. A CogVLM-based quality assessment module filters out low-quality synthetic samples. Experiments on StateFarm and AUC-DDD datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to baseline models and other data augmentation techniques in few-shot settings. The paper explores different ratios of real to synthetic data and evaluates different PCDMs variants.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While the individual components (DWpose, PCDMs, CogVLM) are not novel *per se*, the *integration* of these components into a unified framework specifically tailored for few-shot driver distraction detection is a significant contribution. Prior work has explored data augmentation and GANs for this problem but has often suffered from unstable sample quality and/or high annotation costs. The use of PCDMs to enforce structural consistency based on driver pose and the CogVLM to enforce *semantic consistency* and automatically filter out implausible samples addresses these limitations.
*   **Significance:** Driver distraction detection is an important problem, and this paper directly addresses the critical challenges of data scarcity and domain shift that hinder real-world deployment. By demonstrating significant improvements in performance in few-shot settings, the paper offers a practical and effective approach to improve the generalizability of driver distraction models. This has the potential to make such systems more robust and reliable in real-world vehicles, thus improving traffic safety.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework is well-motivated, addressing a crucial problem in a practical application.
    *   The paper presents a clear and well-structured approach.
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, with comparisons to multiple baselines and ablation studies to validate the contribution of each component.
    *   The results demonstrate a significant performance improvement compared to other data augmentation methods.
    *   The analysis of real-to-synthetic data ratios provides valuable insights for practical implementation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dependence on the accuracy of the pose estimation component (DWpose) is a potential limitation. In scenarios with poor lighting or occlusion, the pose estimation might fail, leading to low-quality synthetic samples.  The paper could have considered the impact of pose estimation error.
    *   While CogVLM is used to filter out samples that are semantically inconsistent, the reliance on English prompts might limit its effectiveness for datasets with drivers speaking different languages or performing actions that are difficult to describe with simple English sentences.
    *   While a good level of ablation analysis is present, a further analysis of the failure modes of the model, particularly regarding the specific types of distraction that are still difficult to detect even with the augmented data, would strengthen the work.
    *   The reliance on commercial datasets, while understandable, limits the reproducibility and access by the wider research community.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a practical and well-engineered solution to a significant problem. The novelty lies in the combination of existing techniques in a novel way, tailored to the specific challenges of few-shot driver distraction detection. The framework is well-motivated and the experimental results are convincing.  The limitations regarding dependence on pose estimation accuracy, language dependence of the quality filter and specific failure modes, prevent a higher score. A score of 7 reflects the solid contribution to the field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Layer-Wise Perturbations via Sparse Autoencoders for Adversarial Text Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10404v1)
- **Authors**: Huizhen Shu, Xuying Li, Qirui Wang, Yuji Kosuga, Mengqiu Tian, Zhuo Li
- **Abstract**: With the rapid proliferation of Natural Language Processing (NLP), especially Large Language Models (LLMs), generating adversarial examples to jailbreak LLMs remains a key challenge for understanding model vulnerabilities and improving robustness. In this context, we propose a new black-box attack method that leverages the interpretability of large models. We introduce the Sparse Feature Perturbation Framework (SFPF), a novel approach for adversarial text generation that utilizes sparse autoencoders to identify and manipulate critical features in text. After using the SAE model to reconstruct hidden layer representations, we perform feature clustering on the successfully attacked texts to identify features with higher activations. These highly activated features are then perturbed to generate new adversarial texts. This selective perturbation preserves the malicious intent while amplifying safety signals, thereby increasing their potential to evade existing defenses. Our method enables a new red-teaming strategy that balances adversarial effectiveness with safety alignment. Experimental results demonstrate that adversarial texts generated by SFPF can bypass state-of-the-art defense mechanisms, revealing persistent vulnerabilities in current NLP systems.However, the method's effectiveness varies across prompts and layers, and its generalizability to other architectures and larger models remains to be validated.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Layer-Wise Perturbations via Sparse Autoencoders for Adversarial Text Generation" introduces a new black-box adversarial attack method, the Sparse Feature Perturbation Framework (SFPF), to generate adversarial texts capable of bypassing security mechanisms in NLP models, particularly LLMs.  The approach utilizes sparse autoencoders (SAEs) to identify and manipulate critical features within text representations. The process involves training an SAE on internal activations of a language model, clustering latent features associated with successful attacks, perturbing hidden states based on these identified features, and reconstructing text using embedding similarity search.  The goal is to balance adversarial effectiveness (jailbreaking the LLM) with safety alignment (reducing harmful outputs). The authors demonstrate that their SFPF approach can bypass state-of-the-art defense mechanisms on certain benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using SAEs for adversarial text generation shows moderate novelty.  Prior work has touched on SAEs for interpretability and defense, but the explicit use of SAE-guided feature perturbation for *attack*, combined with safety alignment considerations, is a distinct contribution. However, the individual components (SAEs, clustering, perturbation, embedding search) are well-established, so the novelty lies primarily in the *integration* of these techniques within a specific adversarial framework. The idea to perturb specific layers is a reasonable extension, but perhaps too obvious.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance lies in providing a more interpretable and controllable method for generating adversarial examples.  This could be valuable for red-teaming and stress-testing LLMs, leading to more robust defense strategies.  The explicit consideration of safety alignment is also a positive aspect, addressing a growing concern in the field. However, the paper's current scope limits its impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The method is well-defined and clearly explained, making the approach reproducible.
    *   The idea of balancing adversarial effectiveness with safety alignment is a crucial consideration in current adversarial research.
    *   The paper presents a comprehensive framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Limited Evaluation Scope:* The experiments are primarily conducted on Qwen3-32B (as the target LLM) and Llama-2-7b-chat-hf (for SAE training).  The generalizability to other architectures and larger models is not thoroughly validated. More rigorous testing is needed across diverse model sizes (e.g. more than just Llama-2 7b) and architectures (e.g. GPT). The claims of generalizability are not convincingly supported by the limited range of model architectures.

    *   *Layer Dependence:* The effectiveness of the method varies significantly depending on the chosen layer for perturbation. The paper acknowledges this, but it raises questions about the robustness and practicality of the approach.  The process of finding the optimal layer appears to be heuristic, lacking a systematic or theoretically grounded method.

    *   *Limited Theoretical Grounding:* The justifications for various design choices (e.g., the specific SAE architecture, the clustering parameters) are somewhat limited. A deeper theoretical analysis of why certain features are more adversarial-sensitive and how the SAE captures these features would strengthen the paper.

    *   *Dependency on Existing Attacks:*  The method is presented as an enhancement to existing attacks (DRA, Adaptive). While this demonstrates its utility, it doesn't fully showcase its standalone capabilities as a novel attack vector. A comparison against a diverse range of recent attacks in similar conditions would be more convincing.

    *   *Concern about Red-Teaming:* The method might not directly translate to better red-teaming strategies if the effectiveness varies drastically across prompts. More analysis is required to ensure applicability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's influence will depend on the extent to which the method can be generalized and scaled. The interpretable nature of the approach could inspire further research into understanding and mitigating adversarial vulnerabilities in LLMs. The safety alignment aspect could contribute to a more responsible approach to adversarial research.
*   **Lack of a Theoretical Understanding:** The paper fails to provide a strong theoretical justification or explanation for the effectiveness of its approach. The authors acknowledge the variations in effectiveness across prompts and layers but offer limited insight. A more comprehensive theoretical framework is needed.

Overall, the paper presents a potentially valuable approach with some interesting features, but the limited evaluation scope, the observed layer dependence, and the lack of a theoretical backbone weaken its impact. The paper's contribution to the field is rather limited.

**Score: 5**

**Rationale:**

The score of 5 reflects the moderate novelty and limited evaluation of the proposed method. While the use of SAEs for adversarial text generation and the consideration of safety alignment are promising aspects, the lack of strong experimental validation across diverse models and the heuristic nature of layer selection limit the significance of the contribution.  The weaknesses outweigh the strengths in the current form, indicating that more substantial research is needed to fully realize the potential of the SFPF approach. The lack of theoretical grounding also limits the score. The current contribution doesn't warrant a high score, even though the direction of research is meaningful.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 5/10

### Translation of Text Embedding via Delta Vector to Suppress Strongly Entangled Content in Text-to-Image Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10407v1)
- **Authors**: Eunseo Koh, Seunghoo Hong, Tae-Young Kim, Simon S. Woo, Jae-Pil Heo
- **Abstract**: Text-to-Image (T2I) diffusion models have made significant progress in generating diverse high-quality images from textual prompts. However, these models still face challenges in suppressing content that is strongly entangled with specific words. For example, when generating an image of ``Charlie Chaplin", a ``mustache" consistently appears even if explicitly instructed not to include it, as the concept of ``mustache" is strongly entangled with ``Charlie Chaplin". To address this issue, we propose a novel approach to directly suppress such entangled content within the text embedding space of diffusion models. Our method introduces a delta vector that modifies the text embedding to weaken the influence of undesired content in the generated image, and we further demonstrate that this delta vector can be easily obtained through a zero-shot approach. Furthermore, we propose a Selective Suppression with Delta Vector (SSDV) method to adapt delta vector into the cross-attention mechanism, enabling more effective suppression of unwanted content in regions where it would otherwise be generated. Additionally, we enabled more precise suppression in personalized T2I models by optimizing delta vector, which previous baselines were unable to achieve. Extensive experimental results demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms existing methods, both in terms of quantitative and qualitative metrics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of suppressing strongly entangled content in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models.  It proposes a novel approach involving a "delta vector" that modifies text embeddings to weaken the influence of undesired content.  The delta vector can be obtained through a zero-shot approach or an optimization-based method. The paper also introduces Selective Suppression with Delta Vector (SSDV), which adaptively utilizes the delta vector within cross-attention to target specific regions.  Experiments demonstrate that the proposed approach outperforms existing methods in both quantitative and qualitative evaluations, including personalized models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in directly manipulating the text embedding space to suppress entangled content.  Existing methods primarily focus on attention manipulation or noise level adjustments, which are less effective when content is strongly associated with specific keywords. The idea of using a "delta vector" to directly influence the text embedding is a solid contribution. The SSDV method, which incorporates this delta vector into the cross-attention mechanism for selective suppression, is a further extension of the initial idea and adds to the paper's overall novelty. The demonstration that delta vector can be effectively applied and optimized for personalized T2I model is also a key novel component since prior works do not address these models.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its ability to address a common limitation of T2I models: the difficulty of removing strongly entangled content. Overcoming this limitation is important for a few reasons.  First, it improves the controllability and flexibility of T2I models, allowing users to generate images that more accurately reflect their desired prompts.  Second, it mitigates the risk of generating undesirable content, which is particularly relevant in preventing copyright infringement or malicious outputs. The results on personalized models are particularly significant, as they address a limitation of existing suppression techniques and provide a path toward more controllable personalized image generation.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Effective Suppression:** Experimental results convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in suppressing strongly entangled content.
    *   **Zero-Shot Capability:** The zero-shot method for obtaining the delta vector is practically useful, as it avoids the need for additional training.
    *   **Selective Suppression:** The SSDV method allows for fine-grained control over content suppression, minimizing unintended effects on other image regions.
    *   **Applicability to Personalized Models:** The successful application of the method to personalized T2I models is a notable achievement.
    *   **Optimization-Based Method:** The introduction of the optimization-based approach further enhances the precision and control over the negative content in personalized T2I models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of Entanglement:** While the paper addresses *strong* entanglement, it doesn't delve into the intricacies of handling more nuanced or complex entanglement scenarios. The examples provided are relatively straightforward (e.g., mustache/Charlie Chaplin, glasses/Steve Jobs).
    *   **Dependency on Mask Generation:** The performance of the optimization-based method for personalized models depends on the accurate generation of the mask indicating the negative content region. This mask generation process itself could be a source of error.
    *   **Computational Cost:** The optimization approach, while effective, likely adds computational overhead compared to the zero-shot method. The paper could benefit from a discussion of the computational costs associated with both approaches.
    *   **Quantitative Evaluation:** The evaluation metrics rely on CLIP score and FID, which are known to have limitations and might not perfectly capture the perceptual quality or semantic accuracy of the generated images.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in T2I models by providing a new perspective on content suppression. The concept of directly manipulating the text embedding space to control image generation could be explored further in other contexts. The delta vector concept could be adapted to address other challenges in T2I models, such as improving compositional reasoning or enhancing attribute control.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and effective method for suppressing strongly entangled content in T2I diffusion models.  The zero-shot and selective suppression aspects enhance its practicality and control, while the application to personalized models expands its relevance.  While the paper demonstrates a strong performance, some weaknesses exist. The limitations on the entanglement scope, the reliance on mask generation for optimized personalization, the potential computational costs, and the shortcomings in the quantitative assessment of perceptual quality do introduce some drawbacks. However, the core idea is sound, and the results are compelling, indicating that this approach can be a significant advancement within the T2I field. Therefore, a score of 7.5 reflects the balance between significant contribution and identified limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating LLMs on Chinese Idiom Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10421v1)
- **Authors**: Cai Yang, Yao Dou, David Heineman, Xiaofeng Wu, Wei Xu
- **Abstract**: Idioms, whose figurative meanings usually differ from their literal interpretations, are common in everyday language, especially in Chinese, where they often contain historical references and follow specific structural patterns. Despite recent progress in machine translation with large language models, little is known about Chinese idiom translation. In this work, we introduce IdiomEval, a framework with a comprehensive error taxonomy for Chinese idiom translation. We annotate 900 translation pairs from nine modern systems, including GPT-4o and Google Translate, across four domains: web, news, Wikipedia, and social media. We find these systems fail at idiom translation, producing incorrect, literal, partial, or even missing translations. The best-performing system, GPT-4, makes errors in 28% of cases. We also find that existing evaluation metrics measure idiom quality poorly with Pearson correlation below 0.48 with human ratings. We thus develop improved models that achieve F$_1$ scores of 0.68 for detecting idiom translation errors.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces IDIOMEVAL, a novel framework for evaluating the performance of Large Language Models (LLMs) in translating Chinese idioms. Recognizing the unique challenges posed by idioms due to their figurative meanings and cultural context, the authors create a comprehensive error taxonomy with seven distinct categories of translation failures, including mistranslation, literal translation, and partial translation. They annotate 900 translation pairs generated by nine different systems, including GPT-4 and Google Translate, across four domains (web, news, Wikipedia, social media). Their analysis reveals that existing evaluation metrics correlate poorly with human judgements of idiom translation quality. To address this, they fine-tune Qwen2.5 models for idiom translation error detection, achieving significantly improved performance compared to existing metrics and prompting approaches. The authors provide a detailed analysis of the performance of various models and domains, shedding light on the current limitations of LLMs in handling Chinese idioms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a few key novelties. First, the focus on Chinese idiom translation is relatively underexplored, making this a welcome contribution. Second, the creation of IDIOMEVAL with its comprehensive error taxonomy is valuable. While error taxonomies aren't inherently groundbreaking, the targeted application to Chinese idiom translation is well-motivated. Third, the finding that existing evaluation metrics struggle with idiom translation is important, and the attempt to create better detection models is promising.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in several areas.
    *   *Highlighting a Weakness of LLMs:* The paper convincingly demonstrates a specific weakness in current LLMs: their difficulty with idiomatic translation, particularly in a language like Chinese with a rich idiomatic tradition.
    *   *Providing a Valuable Resource:* IDIOMEVAL provides the community with a valuable resource for evaluating and improving machine translation systems.
    *   *Guiding Future Research:* The paper's findings and analysis can guide future research towards developing more robust and culturally aware MT systems.
    *   *Improving Automatic Evaluation:* The work to improve automatic evaluation could be very important for creating evaluation methods that better capture human judgement for figurative language, which can generalize to other related tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   *Well-defined Problem:* The problem of Chinese idiom translation is well-defined and important.
    *   *Comprehensive Analysis:* The paper presents a comprehensive analysis using a diverse set of models and domains.
    *   *Clear Methodology:* The annotation process and evaluation metrics are clearly described.
    *   *Empirical Evidence:* The paper provides strong empirical evidence to support its claims.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   *Limited Scope of Improvement:* While the fine-tuning of Qwen2.5 shows improvement in *detecting* idiom translation errors, it's not clear how much this directly translates into *better idiom translation*. The paper focuses more on *evaluation* than on directly *improving translation quality*.
    *   *Generality of Error Taxonomy:* Although applied to Chinese, idioms are common across languages. More discussion could have been made about generalizing IDIOMEVAL to other languages to increase impact.
    *   *Incremental Improvement:* The improvement gained with their fine-tuned models is not that significant, so there is still plenty of room for improvement for better error detection models.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the field by:
    *   Encouraging researchers to focus on the challenges of idiomatic translation in MT.
    *   Promoting the development of more nuanced evaluation metrics that better capture human judgements.
    *   Guiding the creation of training datasets and techniques that specifically address idiomatic language.
    * Providing a reference point and baseline for future evaluation of error detection approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a solid contribution to the field of machine translation, particularly by focusing on a relatively neglected but important aspect: Chinese idiom translation. The creation of IDIOMEVAL and the subsequent analysis highlight real weaknesses in current LLMs and evaluation metrics. While the improvement in idiom translation through fine-tuning is somewhat limited (more emphasis is placed on error *detection*), the paper provides a clear path forward for future research. The results will have strong impacts on low resource languages, as creating parallel data can be time-consuming.

Therefore, a score of **7** is justified. The paper presents valuable novelty, a well-defined problem, empirical analysis, and a strong case for improvement of evaluation metrics. The limitations are around the scope of the improvements (error detection rather than direct translation improvement) and a somewhat incremental contribution in the final method.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### NanoControl: A Lightweight Framework for Precise and Efficient Control in Diffusion Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10424v1)
- **Authors**: Shanyuan Liu, Jian Zhu, Junda Lu, Yue Gong, Liuzhuozheng Li, Bo Cheng, Yuhang Ma, Liebucha Wu, Xiaoyu Wu, Dawei Leng, Yuhui Yin
- **Abstract**: Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) have demonstrated exceptional capabilities in text-to-image synthesis. However, in the domain of controllable text-to-image generation using DiTs, most existing methods still rely on the ControlNet paradigm originally designed for UNet-based diffusion models. This paradigm introduces significant parameter overhead and increased computational costs. To address these challenges, we propose the Nano Control Diffusion Transformer (NanoControl), which employs Flux as the backbone network. Our model achieves state-of-the-art controllable text-to-image generation performance while incurring only a 0.024\% increase in parameter count and a 0.029\% increase in GFLOPs, thus enabling highly efficient controllable generation. Specifically, rather than duplicating the DiT backbone for control, we design a LoRA-style (low-rank adaptation) control module that directly learns control signals from raw conditioning inputs. Furthermore, we introduce a KV-Context Augmentation mechanism that integrates condition-specific key-value information into the backbone in a simple yet highly effective manner, facilitating deep fusion of conditional features. Extensive benchmark experiments demonstrate that NanoControl significantly reduces computational overhead compared to conventional control approaches, while maintaining superior generation quality and achieving improved controllability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "NanoControl: A Lightweight Framework for Precise and Efficient Control in Diffusion Transformer" addresses the problem of high computational cost and parameter overhead associated with controllable image generation using Diffusion Transformers (DiTs). Existing methods often rely on the ControlNet paradigm, which duplicates the DiT backbone. NanoControl introduces a lightweight LoRA-style control module that directly learns control signals from raw conditioning inputs. It also employs a KV-Context Augmentation mechanism to integrate condition-specific information into the backbone. The authors claim state-of-the-art controllable image generation performance with a minimal increase in parameter count and GFLOPs. Experiments demonstrate reduced computational overhead and improved controllability compared to conventional approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper's novelty lies in its lightweight approach to controllable DiT image generation.  Instead of duplicating the entire backbone network like ControlNet, NanoControl uses a LoRA-style control module and a novel KV-Context Augmentation mechanism.  While LoRA itself is not new, its application in this specific context of DiT control and the design of the control module with KV-Context Augmentation presents a reasonably innovative combination.  However, one could argue that the idea of using LoRA to reduce the parameter footprint of ControlNet-like methods is relatively intuitive, which somewhat diminishes the perceived novelty. Prior work like OminiControl also uses LoRA, so NanoControl needs to show a significant advancement beyond that.

**Significance:**

The significance of the work stems from its potential to enable controllable image generation on resource-constrained devices. The very small increase in parameter count (0.024%) and computational cost (0.029% GFLOPs) compared to the original Flux model is compelling. If the performance claims hold up, this could have a practical impact on accessibility to controllable generation. The ablation studies help isolate the importance of the KV-Context Augmentation. The results show improvements on a set of standard tasks, and the comparisons with existing methods seem thorough.

**Strengths:**

*   **Lightweight Design:** The key strength is the extremely low parameter and FLOPs overhead.
*   **Competitive Performance:** The paper claims state-of-the-art controllability while maintaining high generation quality, which is a strong result.
*   **KV-Context Augmentation:** The proposed mechanism contributes to efficient and effective conditional feature injection.
*   **Thorough Evaluation:**  The paper presents quantitative and qualitative results comparing NanoControl with several existing methods on various tasks, including at higher resolutions.
